The new Prime Minister and Minister flew to Tokyo almost immediately after being sworn in to attend a [Quadrilateral Security Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrilateral_Security_Dialogue) meeting with fellow world leaders United States president [Joe Biden](145422.md), Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) and Japanese prime minister [Fumio Kishida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fumio_Kishida). At the meeting, Albanese and Wong confirmed that the new government intended to make additional action on climate change a key part of Australia's foreign policy moving forward.[^cite_note-340]

In May 2024, [Radio New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_New_Zealand) reported that the Albanese government had slightly increased Australia's foreign aid budget by four percent, bringing its total 2024–2025 aid to A\$4.961 billion budget. In August 2023, the Government had released its new international development strategy, which promised new country, gender, disability and humanitarian aid strategies.[^cite_note-341]

#### China

Main article: [Australia-China relations § Albanese Government\:2022–present](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia-China_relations#Albanese_Government\:2022%E2%80%93present)



Albanese, US president [Joe Biden](145422.md), and British prime minister [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak) at the AUKUS meeting in San Diego in 2023

On 12 June 2022, [Deputy Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_Australia) and Defence Minister [Richard Marles](4386805.md) held a meeting with Chinese Defence Minister [Wei Fenghe](38755987.md) at the [Shangri-La Dialogue](5410193.md) in Singapore. It was the highest-level contact between Australia and China in almost three years following a deterioration in [Australia-China relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia-China_relations) under the previous Morrison government. The two ministers discussed an incident involving the Chinese interception of a [Royal Australian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force) [Boeing P-8 Poseidon](731169.md) over the [South China Sea](74209.md) and political developments in the Pacific Islands.[^cite_note-342] Earlier, Marles reiterated his government's commitment to pursuing a "productive relationship" with China while advancing its own national interests and regional security within a rules-based system. In response to Chinese concerns that [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) was a "mini-[NATO](21133.md)", Marles stated that AUKUS was not an alliance similar to NATO but rather focused on the "sharing and development of capabilities" between Australia, the United Kingdom, and United States.[^cite_note-343]

In November 2022, Albanese held a bilateral meeting with General Secretary of the Chinese Communist Party [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), bringing an end to the longest diplomatic freeze in 50 years between Australia and China.[^cite_note-344] In December 2022, Australian foreign minister [Penny Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Wong) visited China, the first Australian minister to visit the country since 2019, where she met Chinese foreign minister [Wang Yi](18211536.md).[^cite_note-345] In early 2023, China ended its unofficial ban on imports of Australian coal,[^cite_note-346] with all restrictions reportedly being lifted by 14 March.[^cite_note-347] On 11 April the countries announced that they reached an agreement over disputes over China's barley imports from Australia, with China agreeing to review its tariffs in exchange for Australia suspending a case against China in the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) WTO.[^cite_note-348] These developments were followed by Chinese Vice Foreign Minister [Ma Zhaoxu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma_Zhaoxu)'s visit to Australia in mid-April 2023 and Australia [Minister for Trade and Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Trade_and_Tourism) [Don Farrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Farrell)'s visit to Beijing, which marked the first visits by senior Chinese and Australian visits since 2017 and 2019 respectively.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350] Chinese ambassador to Australia [Xiao Qian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiao_Qian_\(diplomat\)) announced on 18 May that China would allow imports of Australian timber, which was suspended in 2020.[^cite_note-351] This was followed by the ending of China's 80% tariff on Australian [barley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barley) in early August 2023[^cite_note-352] and its tariffs on Australian wine in March 2024.[^cite_note-353]

In September 2023, the Australian and Chinese governments held a high-level dialogue in Beijing, the first in three years, with former trade minister [Craig Emerson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Emerson) leading the Australian delegation, while China was represented by former foreign minister [Li Zhaoxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Zhaoxing). Later that month Albanese met with Chinese premier [Li Qiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Qiang) on the sidelines of the [East Asia Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Asia_Summit) held in Jakarta.[^cite_note-354] On 11 October 2023, Australian journalist [Cheng Lei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheng_Lei_\(journalist\)), a former [CGTN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CGTN) news anchor who was detained in September 2020, was released and returned to Australia.[^cite_note-355] During 4–7 November 2023, Albanese visited Shanghai and Beijing, becoming the first Australian prime minister to visit China in seven years.[^cite_note-356] The trip, described as an effort to improve Australian-Chinese bilateral relations, coincided with the 50th anniversary of Prime Minister Gough Whitlam's first state visit to China.[^cite_note-357] During the trip he gave a speech at the [China International Import Expo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_International_Import_Expo), and met with Premier [Li Qiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Qiang) and CCP General Secretary [Xi Jinping](2017814.md).[^cite_note-358]

In mid-June 2024 Albanese hosted [Chinese Premier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_China) [Li Qiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Li_Qiang) during his state visit to Australia. China agreed to extend visa-free access to Australians visiting China for up to 15 days. The two governments also agreed to provide reciprocal five-year multiple entry visas for tourism, business and visiting family members. In addition, the two governments agreed to hold talks on improving bilateral military relations to avoid incidents such as the 2023 sonar attack on divers from HMAS *Toowoomba*. Despite a largely productive visit, an incident occurred when Chinese officials attempted to obstruct Cheng Lei, who had since become a [Sky News Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sky_News_Australia) journalist.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] Despite the improving of Australian-Chinese bilateral relations during the Albanese government, flashpoints have included Australia's continuation of the AUKUS submarine deal, a sonar attack on [Royal Australian Navy](26327.md) divers from [*HMAS Toowoomba*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMAS_Toowoomba_\(FFH_156\)) by the [Chinese destroyer *Ningbo*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_destroyer_Ningbo_\(139\)) near Japan in November 2023, Australia's agreement to brief New Zealand about AUKUS Pillar 2 developments in February 2024, the targeting of 20 Australian lawmakers involved in the [Inter-Parliamentary Alliance on China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-Parliamentary_Alliance_on_China) (IPAC) by Chinese spies ([APT31](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/APT31)) in April 2024, and a confrontation between a Chinese Air Force jet and Australian military helicopter near [North Korea](21255.md) in May 2024.[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365]

On 13 February 2025, the Australian Defence Department lodged a formal complaint with the [People's Liberation Army](66890.md) following an incident in the [South China Sea](74209.md) on 11 February in which a Chinese fighter jet released flares within 30 metres of an [RAAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAAF) [P-8 Poseidon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P-8_Poseidon) aircraft.[^cite_note-366] On 20 February, the Australian and New Zealand Defence Ministers Marles and [Judith Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Collins) confirmed that the [Australian](67538.md) and [New Zealand militaries](21359.md) were monitoring three Chinese warships that were sailing through [Australia's exclusive economic zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone_of_Australia) near Sydney.[^cite_note-367] In response to [Chinese naval live-fire exercises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Chinese_naval_exercises_in_the_Tasman_Sea) disrupting international flights in the [Tasman Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasman_Sea), Foreign Minister Wong met with Director of the [Office of the Central Foreign Affairs Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Central_Foreign_Affairs_Commission) [Wang Yi](18211536.md) at the [G20](2206608.md) summit in [South Africa](17416221.md) to raise Australian concerns about Chinese naval activities near Australia.[^cite_note-368] On 10 April 2025, Albanese and Marles declined Chinese Ambassador to Australia [Xiao Qian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiao_Qian)'s invitation for Australia to join Beijing in opposing US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s [international tariffs](78448911.md). Albanese said that "Australians would speak for ourselves" while Marles said that Australia "would not be holding China's hand".[^cite_note-369]

#### Europe



Albanese meeting with Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) in Kyiv

On 11 June 2022, Albanese announced that the French defence contractor [Naval Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Group) had agreed to settle the previous [Morrison government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_government)'s 2021 cancellation of the 12 [attack-class submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack-class_submarine) for a €555 million (AU\$830 million)[^cite_note-370] compensation settlement. In response, French Armed Forces Minister [Sébastien Lecornu](54364868.md) welcomed the settlement and stated that France aims to rebuild its relationship with Australia.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372] Later that month, Albanese met with French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) in France.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] On 1 July 2022, Albanese travelled to Ukraine to meet with Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md), making him the first Australian prime minister to make a diplomatic visit to Ukraine. Albanese pledged a further \$100 million in aid to assist with the ongoing [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md).[^cite_note-376] In addition, Albanese and the Australian delegation visited [Kyiv](585629.md) and [Bucha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucha,_Kyiv_Oblast) on 4 July 2022, which had seen fighting with Russian forces.[^cite_note-377]

In early December 2022, Albanese hosted Finnish prime minister [Sanna Marin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanna_Marin) in Sydney; which marked the first state visit by a Finnish head of government to Australia. During the visit, the two leaders discussed several issues including the Australia-[European Union](9317.md) trade agreement, human rights, the Russian invasion of Ukraine, and [climate change](5042951.md) mitigation.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] In early May 2023, Albanese and [Governor-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia) [David Hurley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hurley) attended the [coronation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_of_Charles_III_and_Camilla) of [Charles III](125248.md) in London.[^cite_note-380] A cannon salute was also held in Australia to mark the occasion.[^cite_note-381]

In early July 2023, Albanese visited Germany ahead of the [NATO summit in Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Vilnius_summit). Prior to a scheduled meeting with German chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz), Albanese confirmed a bilateral deal to sell Australian-made armoured vehicles to Germany.[^cite_note-382] In July 2024, Deputy Prime Minister Marles announced a further \$250 million in military assistance, the largest single military package from Australia to Ukraine since the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-383] In late December 2024, the federal government confirmed it was working to locate 32-year-old Australian man Oscar Jenkins after he appeared in a video on pro-[Kremlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremlin) social media accounts purportedly showing him being detained and questioned by Russian soldiers in Ukraine. Jenkins had been serving in the [Ukrainian armed forces](42799055.md).[^cite_note-384] In mid-January 2025, Albanese promised to take "the strongest action possible" against Russia if it could be verified that the Russian military had executed Jenkins.[^cite_note-385]

#### India

Main article: [Australia–India relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93India_relations)



Albanese and Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md), September 2024

Albanese visited [India](14533.md) between 8 and 11 March, where he met Indian prime minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) for the Australia-India Annual Leaders' Summit in [New Delhi](51585.md). The state visit focused on bilateral trade and investment, renewable energy, technology, defence and security cooperation, and educational and cultural ties. Australia and India had previously signed the Australia-India Economic Cooperation and Trade Agreement, which came into force on 29 December 2022.[^cite_note-386] During the visit, Albanese and Modi attended the fourth Australia-India cricket test at [Narendra Modi Stadium](80201506.md) in [Ahmedabad](206337.md), where the two leaders also rode a chariot in a lap of honour.[^cite_note-387] Modi reciprocated with a two-day state visit to Sydney on 22 May 2023 where he met Australian political and business leaders including Albanese.[^cite_note-388]

#### Israel-Palestine

Main articles: [Australia–Israel relations](27679535.md) and [Australia–Palestine relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Palestine_relations)

In October 2022, the Albanese government reversed the previous [Morrison government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_government)'s decision to recognise [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem) as [Israel](9282173.md)'s capital. Foreign Minister Penny Wong reaffirmed that Jerusalem's status should be decided through peace negotiations between Israelis and Palestinians.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] In August 2023, Wong confirmed that Australia would revert to its pre-2014 policy of designating the [West Bank](33209.md), [East Jerusalem](343508.md) and the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) as "[Occupied Palestinian Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupied_Palestinian_Territories)" and the [Israeli settlements](15123.md) there as "illegal".[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392] Following the outbreak of the [Gaza war](74998519.md) on 7 October 2023, the Albanese government defended Israel's right to defend itself and retaliate against Hamas' attack.[^cite_note-393] Wong also called for the release of hostages taken by Hamas.[^cite_note-394] Despite its support for Israel, the Albanese government declined to supply weapons to Israel since the start of the conflict.[^cite_note-Canada_stops-395]

In mid-February 2024, Wong expressed concerns about Israel's planned [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive), describing it as "unjustifiable".[^cite_note-jointpms-396] Albanese, along with Canadian prime minister [Justin Trudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Trudeau) and New Zealand prime minister [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md), subsequently issued a joint statement expressing concerns over the planned Israeli ground offensive.[^cite_note-jointpms-396] In early April 2024, Wong said that the federal government was considering recognising [Palestinian statehood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_statehood) as a means of facilitating a two-state solution for Israel and Palestine.[^cite_note-397] Her comments attracted criticism from the [Executive Council of Australian Jewry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_of_Australian_Jewry) President Daniel Aghon[^cite_note-398] and was welcomed by the president of the [Australia Palestine Advocacy Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_Palestine_Advocacy_Network), [Nasser Mashni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasser_Mashni).[^cite_note-399] In May 2024, the Albanese government voted in favour of a [United Nations General Assembly resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine#Renewed_membership_efforts_and_additional_rights_at_the_UN) that determined that [Palestine](241405.md) met the requirements for United Nations membership.[^cite_note-400] Wong subsequently confirmed that the vote did not mean that Australia would recognise Palestinian statehood but was rather about extending "modest additional rights to participate in United Nations forums". She reiterated that Australia would only recognise Palestine "when we think the time is right" and that a reformed [Palestinian Authority](24093.md), not Hamas, should "lead its people" in a future Palestinian state.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402]

On 1 October 2024, Albanese and Opposition leader [Peter Dutton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Dutton) condemned memorial services held at several [Shiite Muslim](26961.md) mosques in Sydney to commemorate the death of leader of the terrorist group [Hezbollah](13919.md), [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md). In addition, federal police commissioner [Reece Kershaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reece_Kershaw) warned that law enforcement authorities would take action against those displaying Hezbollah or Hamas flags at national pro-Palestinian rallies being held on 6 October to mark the first anniversary of the [2023 Hamas-led attack on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel).[^cite_note-403] On 21 November 2024, the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) issued [arrest warrants](146746.md) for Israel's prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) and Defense Minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant), and Hamas military commander [Mohammed Deif](92534.md) (who was subsequently confirmed to have been killed).[^cite_note-404] Foreign Minister [Penny Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Wong) stated in an [X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_\(Twitter\)) post that the Australian government "respects the independence of the ICC and its important role in upholding international law".[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406]

#### New Zealand

Main article: [Australia–New Zealand relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93New_Zealand_relations)

On 10 June 2022, Albanese hosted New Zealand prime minister [Jacinda Ardern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacinda_Ardern) on her first official visit to Australia since 2020. The two leaders discussed a range of issues including Australia's controversial [Section 501 deportation policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_Act_1958#2014:_Character_test) (which had disproportionately affected [New Zealanders living in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Australians)), growing Chinese influence in the Pacific Islands, climate change, and working with Pacific neighbours.[^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408] On 7 February 2023, Albanese hosted his New Zealand counterpart [Chris Hipkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Hipkins), who had succeeded Ardern in late January 2023. Besides reaffirming [Australian-New Zealand bilateral relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia-New_Zealand_relations), they also discussed the controversial Section 501 deportation policy. Albanese reiterated that his government would revise the deportation policy to take into account individuals' connections to Australia and the length of time they had lived in the country.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] Immigration Minister [Andrew Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Giles) also issued Ministerial Directive 99 requiring Australian immigration officials to consider a non-citizen's community ties and length of time spent in Australia when cancelling a visa.[^cite_note-NZH_30_May_2024-411] This policy change was welcomed by Hipkins and the New Zealand Government.[^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413]

On 22 April 2023, Albanese, Home Affairs Minister [Clare O'Neil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clare_O'Neil), and Giles announced the creation of a new direct pathway to [Australian citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_citizenship) for New Zealand [Special Category Visa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Category_Visa) (SCV) holders, commencing 1 July 2023. Under the policy, SCV holders who have resided in Australia for at least four years and met other residency requirements will be eligible to apply for Australian citizenship without having to apply for permanent residency.[^cite_note-414] In addition, children born in Australia to a New Zealand citizen from 1 July will automatically be eligible for Australian citizenship.[^cite_note-NZH_citizenship_pathway-415] The announcement was welcomed by NZ Prime Minister Hipkins and Oz Kiwi chairperson Joanne Cox for improving [New Zealanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealander_Australian)' access to Australian citizenship, health and social security services.[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-NZH_citizenship_pathway-415] On 26 July 2023, Albanese met with Hipkins as part of the annual Australia-New Zealand leaders' meeting.[^cite_note-417]

In early February 2024, Foreign Minister Wong and Defence Minister Marles hosted their New Zealand counterparts [Winston Peters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Peters) and [Judith Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Collins) for a joint bilateral meeting of foreign and defence ministers in Melbourne. Marles confirmed that Australia would send officials to brief their New Zealand counterparts about [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) Pillar Two, which would focus on advanced military technology including quantum computing and artificial intelligence. New Zealand is not expected to join AUKUS Pillar One due to its [nuclear-free policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_nuclear-free_zone). The two governments also committed to reinforced security cooperation in the Indo-Pacific and increased military integration between the Australian and New Zealand militaries.[^cite_note-418][^cite_note-419] In May 2024, following criticism from opposition politicians that Ministerial Directive 99 had allowed several non-citizens convicted of serious crimes to remain in Australia, Albanese and Giles confirmed that the immigration directive would be revised.[^cite_note-NZH_30_May_2024-411][^cite_note-NZH_31_May_2024-420] On 7 June 2024, Giles issued "Directive 110", which clarified that non-citizens convicted of serious crimes could be deported despite living their whole lives in Australia. In response, New Zealand prime minister [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md) expressed concern that people with little or no connection with New Zealand were being deported back to the country.[^cite_note-421]

#### The Pacific

On 12 January 2023, Albanese became the first foreign leader to address the [National Parliament of Papua New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Parliament_of_Papua_New_Guinea).[^cite_note-422] That same day, he and Papua New Guinea Prime Minister [James Marape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Marape) signed a joint statement pledging that the two countries would reach a new security deal by late April 2023.[^cite_note-423] In mid-June 2024, several Australian cabinet ministers including Marles, Wong and [Pat Conroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Conroy_\(politician\)) attended the 30th Australia-Papua New Guinea Ministerial Forum in [Port Moresby](57387.md).[^cite_note-424] During the visit, the Albanese government announced several initiatives under a bilateral security agreement with PNG.[^cite_note-425] This was followed on 12 December 2024 with the announcement that Australia would fund the addition of a PNG team to the [National Rugby League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rugby_League) from 2028. As part of the funding deal, PNG agreed that Australia would remains its "security partner of choice" and PNG would not sign a military or police security deal with China.[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427]

In late September 2023, Defence Minister [Richard Marles](4386805.md) announced that 500 Australian troops would be relocated to [Townsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Townsville) over the course of six years from 2025 to strengthen the [Australian Army](2795.md)'s ability to conduct operations missions in the Pacific.[^cite_note-428] This announcement was criticised by the city's mayor [Jenny Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jenny_Hill_\(politician\)) who claimed that [Townsville City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Townsville_City_Council) was not consulted about military personnel's housing.[^cite_note-429] In early November 2023, Albanese attended the [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum) annual meeting in the [Cook Islands](7067.md) where he met several leaders including Tuvalan Prime Minister [Kausea Natano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kausea_Natano). Key issues discussed at the meeting included the impact of Australian carbon emissions, climate change, nuclear safety, and ongoing US-China tensions.[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431] On 10 November 2023, Albanese and Natano signed a bilateral pact between Australia and Tuvalu, known as the Falepili Union.[^cite_note-432] Under the terms of the treaty, Tuvalan citizens will be given a "special mobility pathway" that would allow them to live and work in Australia. In addition, Australia will respond to major natural disasters, pandemics or military aggression against Tuvalu. In return, Australia will have a veto power over Tuvalu's security and defence agreements with other countries.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]

On 28 August 2024, the Albanese government's Pacific Policing Initiative (PPI) won the support of member states of the [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum), which Australia is a member. Under the PPI, the Australian federal government will establish a police development and coordination hub in [Brisbane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisbane) for training Pacific police officers. In addition, a "Pacific Police Support Group" will also be established to help with natural disasters, emergencies and other events. Four police training centres of excellence will also be set up across the Pacific, with Australia contributing about A\$400m in infrastructure funding over five years.[^cite_note-ABC_28_Aug_2024-435][^cite_note-Guardian_28_Aug_2024-436] Albanese described the launch of the Pacific Police Initiative as a "great outcome" for Pacific security.[^cite_note-Guardian_28_Aug_2024-436] [Melanesian Spearhead Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melanesian_Spearhead_Group) director-general Leonard Louma and Vanuatuan Prime Minister [Charlot Salwai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlot_Salwai) expressed concern that the PPI could be used by Australian and New Zealand to serve Western interests and lock China out of the region.[^cite_note-ABC_28_Aug_2024-435][^cite_note-Guardian_28_Aug_2024-436] By contrast, Tongan Prime Minister [Siaosi Sovaleni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siaosi_Sovaleni), Fijian prime minister [Sitiveni Rabuka](516580.md), Tuvalan Prime Minister [Feleti Teo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feleti_Teo), and New Zealand prime minister [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md) expressed support for the PPI.[^cite_note-Guardian_28_Aug_2024-436][^cite_note-RNZ_2_Sep_2024-437] Solomon Islands Prime Minister [Jeremiah Manele](10375180.md) expressed cautious support for the Pacific Police Initiative but said that national consultation was important.[^cite_note-RNZ_2_Sep_2024-437]

On 9 December 2024, Albanese signed a joint security treaty with Nauruan President [David Adeang](785296.md) in which Australia agreed to provide Nauru with A\$100 million (US\$64 million) in direct aid over five years in exchange for Nauru consulting Australia before signing any bilateral agreements with other countries.[^cite_note-438]

On 20 December 2024, Albanese signed an A\$190 million (US\$118 million) agreement with Solomon Islands Prime Minister Manele to expand the capabilities of the [Royal Solomon Islands Police Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Solomon_Islands_Police_Force). This funding package would support the Solomon Islands Police Force's funding, training and infrastructure including the construction of a new police training centre in [Honiara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honiara). According to [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) and [Reuters](18998750.md), this funding packaged was intended to compete with expanding Chinese influence in the Pacific region particularly the Solomon Islands, which had entered into a [security agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China%E2%80%93Solomon_Islands_relations#Security_pact) with China in 2022.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440]

#### Southeast Asia

On 6 June 2022, Albanese along with Foreign Minister Wong, Trade Minister [Don Farrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Farrell) and Industry Minister [Ed Husic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Husic) visited Indonesian president [Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo) in [Jakarta](16275.md) to reaffirm relations with Indonesia and [ASEAN](106539.md) (Association of Southeast Asian Nations).[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442] On 3 July 2023, Albanese hosted Indonesian president Widodo during a 36-hour visit to Sydney, where he also met several Australian business and political leaders.[^cite_note-443] On 6 September 2023, Albanese attended the [ASEAN Summit](3350351.md) in [Jakarta](16275.md) to promote trade relations with the region.[^cite_note-444] On 8 September, Albanese met with Philippine president [Bongbong Marcos](572460.md) in [Manila](184334.md), marking the first state visit to the Philippines by an Australian prime minister in two decades. The two governments agreed to conduct joint naval patrols in the [South China Sea](74209.md).[^cite_note-445]

During 4–6 March 2024, Australia hosted a "special summit" between Australia and the member nations of ASEAN to mark 50 years of diplomatic dialogue relations.[^cite_note-446] Several diplomatic and economic initiatives were reached at the summit, including a \$2 billion "Southeast Asia Investment Financing Facility", a \$140 million infrastructure development partnership, and expanded business visa programs.[^cite_note-447] On 15 December 2024 Albanese, Wong and Burke confirmed that surviving [Bali Nine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali_Nine) members [Matthew Norman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Norman), [Scott Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scott_Rush), [Martin Stephens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Stephens_\(drug_smuggler\)), [Si Yi Chen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Si_Yi_Chen), and [Michael Czugaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Czugaj) had been repatriated to Australia after serving over 19 years in prison in Indonesia. The five had been convicted of attempting to import over eight kilograms of [heroin](14034.md) from [Bali](4147.md) in 2005.[^cite_note-448]

#### United States

See also: [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) and [Australia–United States relations § Biden administration (2021–2025)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93United_States_relations#Biden_administration_\(2021%E2%80%932025\))



Albanese and US President [Joe Biden](145422.md), October 2023

In late October 2023, Albanese undertook a four-day state visit to [Washington, D.C.](108956.md), where he met United States president [Joe Biden](145422.md), Australian Ambassador to the United States [Kevin Rudd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Rudd) and several members of the [United States Congress](31756.md). Key objectives of the visit including lobbying Congress to pass [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS)-related legislation, sign new deals on critical minerals and discuss issues with China and the Israel-Hamas War.[^cite_note-449] On 14 December 2023, Congress passed legislation allowing [nuclear-powered submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear-powered_submarine) to be sold to Australia under the AUKUS agreement.[^cite_note-450]

On 21 September 2024, Albanese met with Biden ahead of the annual [Quadrilateral Security Dialogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadrilateral_Security_Dialogue) meeting in [Wilmington, Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmington,_Delaware).[^cite_note-451] Following the [2024 United States presidential election](59568546.md) held on 6 November, Albanese and Opposition leader Dutton congratulated [Republican Party](32070.md) candidate and President-elect [Donald Trump](4848272.md).[^cite_note-452]

#### Other foreign affairs and defence

On 29 September 2023, Marles announced that the [Australian Defence Force](67538.md) would be retiring its fleet of [MRH-90 Taipan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRH-90) helicopters following a fatal crash during [Exercise Talisman Sabre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exercise_Talisman_Sabre) in July 2023 which killed four military personnel.[^cite_note-453] In early January 2024, Albanese ordered an inquiry into whether the previous [Morrison government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison_government) had failed to hand over documents relating to Australia's involvement in the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md) to the [National Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Archives_of_Australia) in 2020 for public release.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455] On 12 September 2024, Marles stripped nine commanding officers who served in the [War in Afghanistan](19666611.md) of their distinguished service medals, implementing the final recommendation of the [Brereton Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brereton_Report) which had found "credible evidence" that Australian soldiers had unlawfully killed 39 people.[^cite_note-456]

In late March 2025, [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that Albanese had pledged A\$74 billion for missile procurement in 2024, which included A\$21 billion to establish a Guided Weapons and Explosive Ordnance Enterprise in Australia. Australia has formed partnerships with companies like [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin), [Kongsberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kongsberg), and [Raytheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raytheon) to acquire long-range strike and hypersonic missiles, with a focus on enhancing defense capabilities in the [Indo-Pacific](330456.md). Despite global supply shortages, local production of missiles, including Kongsberg's [Joint Strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Strike_Missile) and [Naval Strike Missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Strike_Missile), is set to begin in 2027, with 10 Australian suppliers already contracted. Australia is also working to produce [Guided Multiple Launch Rocket Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M270_Multiple_Launch_Rocket_System) (GMLRS) and [High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems](147011.md) (HIMARS) locally, aimed at improving regional defense readiness.[^cite_note-457]

## Second term of government (2025–present)

See also: [Second Albanese ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Albanese_ministry)

In a [landslide victory](41053306.md) and historic comeback, having overturned most of the [opinion polling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling) showing either a smaller majority for the Labor government or a [hung parliament](652214.md),[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] the Labor Party won a second majority government in the [2025 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_federal_election).[^cite_note-Gavin_2025-461] In doing so, Albanese became the first prime minister since [2004](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Australian_federal_election) to win a second consecutive term,[^cite_note-462] as well as the first Labor leader to win re-election since [Bob Hawke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Hawke) in [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Australian_federal_election).[^cite_note-Gavin_2025-461]

### Economy

#### Tax reform

On 12 May 2026, the Albanese government announced, as part of the [2026 budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Australian_federal_budget), changes to [negative gearing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negative_gearing_in_Australia) and the [capital gains tax discount](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_gains_tax_in_Australia), despite a promise at the 2025 election to leave both policies unchanged. It restricted new negative gearing arrangements to newly-built homes, and removed the capital gains tax discount for any assets bought after 1 July 2027. Furthermore, it implemented a minimum tax rate of 30% on family trusts.[^cite_note-463] The policy measures were criticised by Shadow Treasurer [Tim Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Wilson_\(Australian_politician\)) and [One Nation](83013.md) leader [Pauline Hanson](69926.md), with reserved support from [Greens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Greens) leader [Larissa Waters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larissa_Waters).[^cite_note-464] The legislation passed Parliament with the support of the Greens, who negotiated the addition of a ban on self-managed superannuation funds from borrowing to invest in residential property.[^cite_note-465]

#### Allegations of pork-barrelling

In September 2026, a [Centre for Public Integrity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_Public_Integrity_\(Australia\)) (CPI) investigation made significant allegations of [pork-barreling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pork_barrel) against the Albanese government, through its Major and Local Community Infrastructure (MLCI) Program. The CPI found that, of the over 220 organisations invited to apply, 73% were located in Labor-held seats. CPI executive director Catherine West stated that "for every dollar spent in a Coalition seat, \$3.20 was spent in a notionally ALP-held seat".[^cite_note-466] In particular, the Marrickville Golf Club, located in Albanese's seat of [Grayndler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_of_Grayndler), received a [A\$](81215.md)6 million grant as part of the MLCI program. Albanese also held an honorary membership to the club, which was not declared on his [register of interests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Register_of_interests).[^cite_note-467]

### 2025 Bondi Beach shooting

Following the [2025 Bondi Beach shooting](81839099.md) on 14 December which targeted a [Hanukah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanukah) gathering, Albanese vowed to "eradicate" [antisemitism in Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_Australia) and announced plans to introduce new tougher [gun laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_laws_in_Australia) limiting the number of weapons.[^cite_note-468] On 15 December, the [National Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Cabinet) unanimously agreed to strengthen gun laws. Proposals brought forward during the meeting included restricting firearm ownership to Australian citizens only, accelerating the launch of a national firearms register, limiting the number of firearms a single person can own, the duration of firearms licenses and further restricting the types of legal weapons.[^cite_note-469] On 19 December, the Albanese government also confirmed plans to launch a [gun buyback program](81874062.md).[^cite_note-470]

On 18 December, the Albanese government confirmed that they would adopt all the recommendations of the government's special envoy to combat antisemitism [Jillian Segal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jillian_Segal)'s "Plan to Combat Antisemitism". To implement the plan, the [National Security Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_Committee_\(Australia\)) agreed to introduce legislation creating an aggravated [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech) offence targeting preachers and leaders promoting violence, increasing penalties for hate speech-related violence, designating hate speech as an aggravating factor in sentencing for online threats and harassment, developing a blacklist of "hate groups", creating a new federal offence related to racial vilification and advocating racial supremacy and giving the [Home Affairs Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Home_Affairs_\(Australia\)) new powers to cancel visas or deny entry to individuals spreading hate.[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472]

On 21 December, Albanese announced an Independent Commonwealth Review into Australia's federal law enforcement and intelligence agencies to be led by retired public servant [Dennis Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Richardson_\(public_servant\)). The review was expected to deliver a public report on the attack by late April 2026.[^cite_note-473] On 29 December, the review's terms of reference were released.[^cite_note-474] The Albanese government initially ruled out holding a federal [royal commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_commission) into the Bondi Beach shootings, with Home Affairs Minister Tony Burke saying that it was not the best format for national security issues.[^cite_note-475] However, by 8 January 2026, mounting political and public pressure resulted in the establishment of the [Royal Commission on Antisemitism and Social Cohesion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Commission_on_Antisemitism_and_Social_Cohesion), that incorporated the Richardson review, to report by 14 December 2026.[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-477]

#### Hate speech laws

On 12 January 2026, Albanese announced that the Australian Parliament would reconvene on 19 January to introduce fast-tracked hate speech and firearms legislation. He confirmed that the Labor federal government would seek cross-party support from the [Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_\(Australia\)) and [Australian Greens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Greens).[^cite_note-478]

The [bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_\(law\)) passed both houses of parliament on 20 January 2026,[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-480] It passed in the [House](61565.md) 116 votes to 7,[^cite_note-481] and 38–22 in the [Senate](165705.md).[^cite_note-482]

As of May 2026, there is a constitutional challenge from the [National Socialist Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Network) related to the [constitutionality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Australia) of the 2026 hate speech and hate group legislation.[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484]

### Disaster relief

Following the [2025 New South Wales floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_New_South_Wales_floods) in May 2025, Emergency Minister [Kristy McBain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristy_McBain) announced on 22 May that the [Australian Defence Force](67538.md) personnel would be deployed for search and rescue operations in [Taree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taree).[^cite_note-485] By 24 May, the [National Emergency Management Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Emergency_Management_Agency_\(Australia\)) was working to restore [food supply](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_supply), telecommunications and power to flood-affected communities.[^cite_note-486]

### Education

On 31 July 2025, the Albanese government passed legislation in the Australian Parliament reducing student loan debt by 20 percent. The Universities Accord Bill covers several university and [TAFE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAFE) student loans including the [Higher Education Loan Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_education_fees_in_Australia), [VET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_education_and_training) Student Loan and the Australian Apprenticeship Support Loan.[^cite_note-487]

In mid November 2025, *[The Guardian Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian_Australia)* reported that the [Australian Skills Quality Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Skills_Quality_Authority?action=edit\&redlink=1) (ASQA) had cancelled the registrations of 11 vocational training organisations and voided about 30,000 diplomas, certificates and statements of attainment since late 2024. The ASQA also deregistered four educational providers and investigated 144 other providers for "serious matters". In 2023, the Albanese government had allocated A\$37.8 million to improving the quality of vocational training and educational programmes, including establishing a new enforcement unit at the ASQA and a new tip line. This crackdown was part of the federal government's ongoing efforts to combat unscrupulous vocational [diploma mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diploma_mill) providing dubious courses, accreditation, and so-called "fast-track skilled migration pathways".[^cite_note-488]

### Energy

On 30 March 2026, Albanese confirmed that the Australian government would reduce the [fuel excise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuel_excise) rate from 52.6 cents to 26.3 cents per litre between 1 April and 30 June to address rising fuel prices caused by the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), and encourage people to use public transportation. State and federal leaders also agreed to reduce the heavy vehicle road user charge during that period to help truck drivers.[^cite_note-489]

### Gambling

In April 2026, Albanese announced that the government would introduce a cap of three gambling advertisements per hour and prohibit gambling advertising on sports jerseys. Ads will be capped from 6\:30 am to 8\:30 pm and will be banned during school pick up and drop off times. Gambling ads will also be banned on social media unless users are over 18 and are able to opt out of these notifications. Gambling ads featuring celebrities or athletes will also be banned. Ads containing odds details will also be banned. Gambling ads at sports venues will also be banned.[^cite_note-490]

### Indigenous affairs

On 4 March 2026, the Albanese government launched a federal parliamentary inquiry into racism, hate and violence against [Indigenous Australians](12598742.md) in response to the [2026 Invasion Day protest bombing attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Invasion_Day_protest_bombing_attempt), which occurred in [Perth](24355.md) on [Australia Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_Day).[^cite_note-491]

### Internet and social media

In late July 2025, Google warned that it would sue the Australian government if YouTube was included under the government's [social media ban](78404606.md) for children below 16 years.[^cite_note-492] On 30 July, the Albanese government that it would extend its social media age limit to include YouTube following advice from eSafety commissioner [Julie Inman Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Inman_Grant).[^cite_note-493]

On 26 November 2025, the Digital Freedom Project announced it would commence legal action in the High Court against the new laws, saying they violate the [implied right to political communication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_political_communication) in the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Australia).[^cite_note-Evans-230]

On 10 December 2025, the [Online Safety Amendment (Social Media Minimum Age) Act 2024](78404606.md) came into effect, requiring social media to implement age verification systems with the goal of restricting access to minors under the age of 16.[^cite_note-494]

On 31 March 2026, [Communications Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Communications_\(Australia\)) [Anika Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anika_Wells) announced that the government would investigate social media platforms [Facebook](7529378.md), [Instagram](31591547.md), [Snapchat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snapchat), [TikTok](56822861.md) and [YouTube](3524766.md) for potential violations of the under-16 social media ban.[^cite_note-495]

On 15 July 2026, Albanese announced that a specialist "[Office of AI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_AI?action=edit\&redlink=1)" would be created with the [Department of the Prime Minister and Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_the_Prime_Minister_and_Cabinet_\(Australia\)) to oversee the development of [artificial intelligence](1164.md) standards and regulate the environmental and energy impacts of [data centres](579730.md).[^cite_note-496]

### Foreign affairs, trade and defence



Albanese and British Prime Minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md), June 2025



Albanese with Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) and South African President [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md), June 2025

#### China and East Asia

In July 2025, Albanese went on a six-day visit to [China](5405.md).[^cite_note-497] The visit include a trip to the [Great Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China) and a panda breeding center, which drew criticism from the [Coalition](1051313.md).[^cite_note-ABC_News_17_Jul_2025-498] Albanese responded by saying that the visits were intended to show respect to China.[^cite_note-ABC_News_17_Jul_2025-498] According to critics, Albanese ignored the issue of [human rights in China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China), including human rights violations in [Hong Kong](13404.md), [Xinjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang), and [Tibet](31516.md), and focused only on trade.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500]

On 4 May 2026, Albanese and [Japanese Prime Minister](24833.md) [Takaichi Sanae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takaichi_Sanae) signed four agreements in Canberra to boost bilateral cooperation in the areas of energy, defence and critical minerals and a joint declaration on economic security cooperation. The critical minerals agreement identifies six rare earth strategic projects to diversify supply chains including the [Lynas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynas) rare earth project in [Kalgoorlie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalgoorlie) and [Alcoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcoa)'s Gallium Recovery Project.[^cite_note-501]

#### Europe

On 23 March 2026, Albanese and the [President of the European Commission](24468.md) [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) signed a [free trade agreement](2533562.md) following eight years of negotiations. The two leaders also announced a new defence partnership between Australia and the [European Union](9317.md).[^cite_note-502]

#### Israel–Palestine

Main articles: [Australia–Israel relations](27679535.md) and [Australia–Palestine relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Palestine_relations)

In May 2025, Albanese echoed criticisms from other Western nations in demanding Israel allow the supply of [humanitarian aid](76066918.md) into the [Gaza Strip](12047.md), condemning Israel's actions as "completely unacceptable" and an "outrage".[^cite_note-503] However, he opposed [sanctions against Israel](43473050.md) over the [blockade of Gaza](75013719.md), saying he was focusing on "peace and security for both Israelis and Palestinians" rather than "soundbites".[^cite_note-504]

&#x20;On 11 June 2025, Foreign Minister Wong announced that Australia would join New Zealand, [Canada](5042916.md), the [United Kingdom](31717.md) and [Norway](21241.md) in banning and freezing the assets of two far-right Israeli government ministers [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md) and [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md), stating:&#x20;

> Extremist rhetoric advocating the forced displacement of Palestinians and the creation of new Israeli settlements is appalling and dangerous. These actions are not acceptable. We have engaged the Israeli Government on this issue extensively, yet violent perpetrators continue to act with encouragement and immunity. Today's measures are targeted towards individuals who in our view undermine Israel's own security and its standing in the world.[^cite_note-ABC_News_Israeli_ministers-505]

The joint travel bans and sanctions were condemned by Israeli foreign minister [Gideon Sa'ar](485357.md), United States Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md), Ben-Gvir and Smotrich as hostile actions against Israel. Albanese defended his government's response, stating that Australia was concerned about humanitarian issues in Gaza. The Coalition's foreign affairs spokesperson [Michaelia Cash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michaelia_Cash) expressed concern about the travel ban being part of a "pattern of decisions by the Albanese government targeting the Israeli government".[^cite_note-ABC_News_Israeli_ministers-505]

In late July 2025, the Albanese government joined 15 other countries including France, Canada and New Zealand in signing the "New York Call" which proposed recognising Palestinian statehood at the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) in September 2025. This announcement was criticised by both the US and Israeli governments for "rewarding" Hamas' terrorism.[^cite_note-506] On 9 August, Foreign Minister Wong joined her German, Italian, New Zealand and British counterparts in issuing a joint statement condemning Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md)'s plan to militarily occupy [Gaza City](55387.md).[^cite_note-507] That same day, Albanese and New Zealand prime minister [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md) called for a ceasefire in Gaza and opposed Israeli plans to occupy Gaza City.[^cite_note-ABC_News_9_Aug_2025-508]

On 11 August 2025, Albanese announced plans to recognise the State of Palestine at the United Nations in September.[^cite_note-509]

In August 2025, the [Greens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Greens) urged the Albanese government to impose direct sanctions on high-ranking members of Netanyahu's government and to stop supplying parts for [F-35 fighter jets](11812.md) to the global supply chain that can be accessed by Israel.[^cite_note-510] Greens senator [David Shoebridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Shoebridge) said: "If the Albanese government stopped the export of F-35 fighter jet parts to Israel, then their F-35 fleet would be grounded."[^cite_note-511] According to [Amnesty International](18947898.md), by participating in the production of the F-35, Australia violated the [Arms Trade Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_Trade_Treaty).[^cite_note-512]

#### Iran

Main article: [Australia–Iran relations](36971672.md)

See also: [2024 Iranian operations inside Australia](80881934.md)

In June 2025, Albanese and Foreign Minister Wong voiced support for [US strikes on Iranian nuclear sites](80262844.md).[^cite_note-513]

On 26 August 2025, Albanese declared the Iranian Ambassador Ahmad Sadeghi and three other Iranian officials [persona non grata](521241.md) after an investigation by the [Australian Security Intelligence Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Security_Intelligence_Organisation) found that the [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps was responsible for](80881934.md) attacking a Jewish restaurant in [Sydney](27862.md) and a [Melbourne](17306237.md) synagogue in late 2024. Albanese also confirmed that Australia had withdrawn its diplomats from [Tehran](57654.md) and would designate the IRGC as a terrorist organisation. Wong said that this was the first time since World War II that Australia had expelled an ambassador.[^cite_note-514]

On 21 October 2025, the office of the [Attorney-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney-General_of_Australia) issued a provisional transparency notice designating the [Iranian state media](819903.md) outlet [Press TV](10945963.md) as a foreign government entity.[^cite_note-Transparency_notice_21_Oct_2025-515][^cite_note-The_Australian_21_Oct_2025-516] The designation was finalised on 18 November 2025,[^cite_note-Transparency_notice_21_Oct_2025-515] and was formally listed on the Foreign Influence Transparency Scheme register.[^cite_note-The_Australian_21_Oct_2025-516]

On 27 November 2025, the Australian Parliament formally designated the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps as a [state sponsor of terrorism](317645.md), under the framework of the Criminal Code Amendment (State Sponsors of Terrorism) Act 2025.[^cite_note-517][^cite_note-518]

During the [2025-2026 Iranian protests](81976180.md), the Australian embassy in Iran ceased its operations. Australian citizens were urged by the government to leave Iran as soon as possible due to the violent crackdowns and arrests by Iranian security forces.[^cite_note-519] Following the outbreak of the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) on 28 February 2026, Albanese along with Wong and Marles expressed support for US and Israeli strikes, also stating Australia stood with "the brave people of Iran in their struggle against oppression".[^cite_note-520]

On 10 March 2026, the federal government [granted humanitarian visas](82634971.md) to five members of the [Iran women's national football team](9896170.md) competing in the [2026 AFC Women's Asian Cup](67991141.md) amid fears for their safety during the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) and their refusal to sing the [Iranian national anthem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_national_anthem) during a match against [South Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Korea_women's_national_football_team).[^cite_note-521] Two more Iranian footballers subsequently sought asylum in Australia, bringing the total number to seven.[^cite_note-522] That same day, Albanese confirmed that Australia would deploy a long-range reconnaissance aircraft and air-to-air missiles to the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) to help the [Gulf States](220950.md) defend themselves against Iranian missiles.[^cite_note-523]

#### Repatriation of ISIS-linked individuals

In early December 2025, *[The Guardian Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian_Australia)* reported that the Albanese government had declined an American offer to help repatriate about 40 Australian citizens from camps near the Syrian-Turkish border including [Roj camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roj_camp). These individuals are the wives, widows and children of dead and imprisoned [Islamic State](9087364.md) fighters. In June 2025, Home Affairs Minister Tony Burke had met with [Save the Children](436885.md) CEO Mat Tinkler and repatriate advocate Kamalle Dabboussy, who expressed concerns that these individuals were being radicalised. In August 2025, Tinkler and Dabboussy confirmed that the US government had offered to facilitate the repatriation of the 40 Australians. While the Australian government has previously repatriated several nationals from Syria in 2019 and 2022, the Albanese government had declined the American offer due to concerns of a backlash from certain constituents during the lead-up to the [2025 Australian federal election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_federal_election).[^cite_note-524]

On 17 February 2026, Albanese said that the federal government would not help repatriate ISIS-linked families in Syria, stating "if you make your bed, you lie in it." Home Affairs Minister Burke also confirmed the federal government was seeking advice on whether the threshold for imposing temporary exclusion orders had been met. In mid-February 2026, a group of 34 Australian nationals including 11 women and 23 children, had left the Al-Roj refugee camp with the intention of traveling to [Damascus](8914.md) to return to Australia. They had been blocked from leaving [Syrian Democratic Forces](48205248.md)-controlled territory by Syrian government forces. Opposition Liberal Senator [Sarah Henderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Henderson) advocated blocking the return of ISIS-linked adults, citing public safety concerns.[^cite_note-525]

In late April 2026, a group of four Australian women and nine children left the Al-Roj refugee camp and headed to Damascus with the intention of returning to Australia. According to a Syrian official, their repatriation had been delayed by the Australian government for two weeks while they "put procedures in place" to receive the repatriates. On 6 May, the Albanese government confirmed that the group had booked tickets and were expected to arrive in Australia the following day.[^cite_note-526] The group arrived at [Sydney](266313.md) and [Melbourne Airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne_Airport) on 7 May.[^cite_note-527] Police charged two of the women in Melbourne with crime against humanity relating to slavery while a third woman who arrived in Sydney was charged with entering a prohibited area and bineg a member of a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-528][^cite_note-529]

#### Southeast Asia

On 15 May 2025, Albanese visited Indonesian president [Prabowo Subianto](2829084.md) in Jakarta, making his first overseas trip following the 2025 federal election. The state visit sought to shore up [Australia–Indonesia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Indonesia_relations) in the areas of food security, energy, trade, and critical minerals as well as personal relations between the two national leaders. Albanese received an official state reception from Subianto at [Merdeka Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merdeka_Palace) in Jakarta.[^cite_note-ABC_America_15_May_2025-530][^cite_note-Reuters_15_May_2025-531] During the visit, Albanese encouraged Indonesia to pursue closer defence cooperation with Australia while Subianto encouraged Australian investment in the Indonesian economy. Albanese also stated that Indonesia had ruled out allowing the Russians to establish a military base in [Western New Guinea](1432822.md).[^cite_note-Reuters_15_May_2025-531]

In mid November 2025, Albanese hosted President Prabowo during his first state visit to Sydney. On 12 November, the two leaders confirmed that they would sign a [new defence treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia-Indonesia_Security_Agreements) in January 2026 that would commit the two governments to consulting on both leadership and ministerial levels on security matters.[^cite_note-ABC_News_12_Nov_2025-532][^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_12_Nov_2025-533] The treaty builds upon the 2024 Defence Cooperation Agreement,[^cite_note-ABC_News_12_Nov_2025-532] and is also based on a cancelled 1995 bilateral peace agreement signed between Australian prime minister [Paul Keating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Keating) and Indonesian president [Suharto](217513.md).[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_12_Nov_2025-533] Albanese and Prabowo formally signed the bilateral security treaty on 6 February 2026.[^cite_note-534]

In April 2026, Albanese visited Singapore, Brunei and Malaysia to strengthen economic and energy cooperation with those countries in response to energy shortages caused by the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md). In return for obtain for obtaining access to fuel and fertiliser from Malaysia and Brunei, Australian has agreed supply gas and food.[^cite_note-Pearlman_17_Apr_2026-535][^cite_note-536] On 10 April, Albanese met with [Singaporean Prime Minister](350647.md) [Lawrence Wong](32336575.md) and signed a bilateral agreement to meet each other's energy needs, with Singapore agreeing to continue supplying refined fuel in return for Australia supplying [liquefied natural gas](832128.md) (LNG).[^cite_note-537] On 15 April, Albanese met with [Sultan of Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultan_of_Brunei) [Hassanal Bolkiah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassanal_Bolkiah) and the two leaders signed an agreement to maintain open trade and to protect energy supply chains particularly Bruneian diesel and fertiliser exports to Australia.[^cite_note-538] On 16 April, Albanese met with [Malaysian Prime Minister](496731.md) [Anwar Ibrahim](19264815.md). The Malaysian oil and gas company [Petronas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petronas) agreed to provide Australia with excess fuel supplies in return for Australia prioritising its LNG exports to Malaysia.[^cite_note-Pearlman_17_Apr_2026-535][^cite_note-539]

#### New Zealand

Main article: [Australia–New Zealand relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93New_Zealand_relations)

Between 9 and 10 August 2025, Albanese visited New Zealand prime minister [Christopher Luxon](62303596.md) in [Queenstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queenstown,_New_Zealand) for an annual bilateral head of government meeting. During the visit, the two heads of government reiterated calls for a ceasefire in the [Gaza war](74998519.md) and condemned Israel's plan to occupy [Gaza City](55387.md). The two heads of government also discussed various bilateral trade and defence issues including the Gaza war, relations with China and Australia's [501 deportation policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_Act_1958#2014:_Character_test).[^cite_note-ABC_News_9_Aug_2025-508][^cite_note-ODT_11_Aug_2025-540][^cite_note-The_Guardian_10_Aug_2025-541] The two leaders also laid wreaths at a memorial to fallen [ANZAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANZAC) soldiers in [Arrowtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrowtown) on 10 August.[^cite_note-The_Guardian_10_Aug_2025-541]

In early December 2025, Treasurer [Jim Chalmers](40746506.md) and [Minister for Climate Change and Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Climate_Change_and_Energy) [Chris Bowen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Bowen) met with their New Zealand counterparts [Nicola Willis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicola_Willis) and [Simon Watts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Watts) to discuss strengthening bilateral cooperation on climate action, clean energy and related regulations.[^cite_note-542]

On 17 March, Foreign Minister Wong and Defence Minister Marles hosted their New Zealand counterparts [Winston Peters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Peters) and [Judith Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Collins) during the third annual Australia and New Zealand Foreign and Defence Ministers' Meeting in Canberra to discuss foreign policy and defence issues of interests to both countries. The four ministers issued a joint statement reaffirming bilateral cooperation in the Pacific and global security challenges, and criticising Chinese assertiveness in the [South China Sea](74209.md) and Russian aggression against Ukraine.[^cite_note-543] In response, the Chinese Embassy in New Zealand criticised the New Zealand and Australian governments for interfering in Chinese internal affairs, their perceived silence on US military actions during the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) and their alleged "poor records" on human rights and ethnic minorities.[^cite_note-RNZ_19_Mar_2026-544]

#### The Pacific

Further information: [Australia–Papua New Guinea relations § Pukpuk Treaty](16331352.md#Pukpuk_Treaty), and [Australia–Fiji relations](38240546.md)

On 29 August 2025, the Australian and Nauruan governments signed an agreement with Nauru agreeing to accept several formerly detained people without valid Australian visas to the islands in exchange for financial compensation of at least A\$408 million.[^cite_note-545]

On 14 September 2025, the Australian and Papuan governments announced plans to sign a landmark defence treaty that would integrate the two countries' defence forces on 15 September, which would coincide with the 50th anniversary of Papua New Guinea's independence. This proposed agreement would allow Australians and Papuans to serve in each other's militaries, allowing Papuans to gain Australian citizenship.[^cite_note-546][^cite_note-AJE_16_Sep_2025-547] On 16 September, Albanese said that Australia would contribute funds to the construction of a new ministerial wing for the [National Parliament of Papua New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Parliament_of_Papua_New_Guinea).[^cite_note-AJE_16_Sep_2025-547] By 18 September, the two governments had still not yet signed the defence treaty after Papuan Prime Minister [James Marape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Marape)'s cabinet was unable to reach a quorum. Instead, the Australian and Papuan governments signed a communique and committed to signing the treaty within the following weeks.[^cite_note-548]

On 19 September, the Chinese Embassy in Papua New Guinea expressed concern that the Australian-Papuan defence treaty would affect Papua New Guinea's ability to conduct foreign relations with third parties.[^cite_note-549] The Papua New Guinea cabinet subsequently approved the treaty on 2 October.[^cite_note-550] Marape and Albanese signed the treaty in Canberra on 6 October 2025.[^cite_note-551]

On 29 June 2026, Albanese and [Vanuatuan Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Vanuatu) [Jotham Napat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jotham_Napat) signed a bilateral economic and security agreement known as the [Nakamal Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakamal_Agreement) barring the establishment of any foreign military base on the island state.[^cite_note-Blackburn_29_Jun_2026-552]

On 6 July, Albanese and [Fijian Prime Minister](471842.md) [Sitiveni Rabuka](516580.md) signed a formal defence treaty known as the [Ocean of Peace Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_of_Peace_Alliance) and an economic agreement known as the [Vuvale Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuvale_Union?action=edit\&redlink=1) that would see Australia invest A\$1 billion into the Fijian economy and allow more [Fijian citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fijian_nationality_law) to come to Australia. These agreements were part of the Albanese government's efforts to combat Chinese influence in the Pacific region.[^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554] In response to the Ocean of Peace Alliance, [Chinese Foreign Ministry](7453497.md) spokeswoman [Mao Ning](71679066.md) urged Australia to respect the independence of Pacific island nations and "to avoid targeting or harming the interests of any third parties".[^cite_note-555] Hours after the Australian-Fijian defence treaty, the Chinese military tested a long-range ballistic missile in the Pacific Ocean, triggering protests from Australia, New Zealand and Japan. In response, Defence Minister Marles and Foreign Minister Wong expressed concern about any actions which undermined peace and stability in the Pacific region.[^cite_note-556]

#### United States

See also: [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) and [Australia–United States relations § Second Trump administration (2025–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93United_States_relations#Second_Trump_administration_\(2025%E2%80%93present\))

On 25 July 2025, Agriculture Minister [Julie Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Collins) confirmed that Australia would relax biosecurity restrictions on US beef imports. This announcement was welcomed by both US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Secretary of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_Agriculture) [Brooke L. Rollins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooke_L._Rollins) as a "major trade breakthrough" for US beef exporters.[^cite_note-557]

On 21 October 2025, Albanese met with Trump at the [White House](33057.md). The two leaders signed a [rare earths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare_earths) agreement aimed at countering Chinese control over the supply of critical minerals. Trump also reiterated the United States' commitment to supplying [nuclear-powered submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SSN-AUKUS) to Australia as part of the [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) agreement.[^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559] During the White House visit, Trump rebuked Australian Ambassador [Kevin Rudd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Rudd) for making critical social media remarks about him in the past. Following the press conference, Rudd personally apologised to Trump. While Albanese and veteran [Republican](32070.md) [Michael McCaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_McCaul) expressed confidence in Ambassador Rudd, [Opposition Leader](579586.md) [Sussan Ley](577186.md) called on Rudd to resign as Ambassador to the United States.[^cite_note-560]

In late December 2025, Foreign Minister Penny Wong voiced support for [US airstrikes](83205369.md) against Islamic State forces in [Nigeria](21383.md) that occurred on [Christmas Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_Day).[^cite_note-561]

In January 2026, Albanese called for "dialogue and diplomacy" following a [U.S. military operation in Venezuela](82021123.md) that resulted in the capture of President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md).[^cite_note-562]

#### Defence and security cooperation

On 13 July 2025, [Minister for Defence Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Defence_Industry) [Pat Conroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Conroy_\(politician\)) stated that Australia would not commit military forces to any conflict involving Taiwan during an interview with the [Australian Broadcasting Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Broadcasting_Corporation). The previous day, the *[Financial Times](136566.md)* had reported that [Elbridge Colby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbridge_Colby), the [Under Secretary of Defense for Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Under_Secretary_of_Defense_for_Policy), had been pressing Australian and Japanese officials on what they would do in the event of a conflict between China and Taiwan.[^cite_note-563] In response, the Shadow Defence Minister [Angus Taylor](40520884.md) said that Australia should make "principled commitments to Taiwan's security and be prepared to act under certain circumstances".[^cite_note-564]

On 26 July 2025, the Australian Foreign and Defence Ministers Wong and Marles signed the [Geelong Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geelong_Treaty) with their British counterparts, [Foreign Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Secretary_\(United_Kingdom\)) [David Lammy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lammy) and [Secretary of State for Defence](302332.md) [John Healey](434625.md). The Geelong Treaty is a 50-year bilateral defence agreement to facilitate cooperation on the construction of Australia's nuclear-powered [SSN-AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SSN-AUKUS) submarine fleet under Phase I of the [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) pact.[^cite_note-565]

On 28 May 2026, the Australian government filed a A\$2 billion lawsuit against the American multinational conglomerate [3M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3M), alleging that the company made false statements about the long-term environmental impact of firefighting foam containing [PFAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFAS) that was used at 28 [Australian Defence Force](67538.md) sites. This lawsuit marks the largest legal claim brought by the Australian government. In response, 3M said that it would fight the lawsuit.[^cite_note-566]
